# Meherr BUILD-STATE — claim ledger + protocol

> Machine-maintained by the careyield-build-loop. One row per week-unit. This file IS the lock.

## Claim protocol (every loop session follows this exactly)

1. `git pull --rebase` the working branch (see **Home resolution** below) so this file is current.
2. Pick ONE unit, in this priority order:
   a. an `in-progress` unit whose continuation notes you can finish;
   b. a `claimed`/`in-progress` unit that the **staleness rule** below says you may reclaim;
   c. the lowest-numbered `available` unit whose dependencies are `done` (units marked `[P]` in the plan are claimable out of order).
3. Claim it: set status `claimed`, your session id (short), and UTC timestamp in this table. **Commit and push this claim edit immediately, before building.**
3b. **Validate your own timestamp before you write it.** A claim stamped in the FUTURE is never
   reclaimable — W54's rule refuses to steal live claims on clock skew — so a typo (observed
   2026-08-10: W151 stamped `2026-08-11T13:20Z`) plus a dying holder equals a row locked forever.
   That one self-closed; the next might not. Stamp the real UTC time, and if you find a
   future-dated claim whose holder is demonstrably FAILED, correct the timestamp to its real
   claim time first and then apply the normal rule — repairing a dead row, never stealing a live one.
4. If the push is rejected (another session claimed simultaneously): `git pull --rebase`, pick the *next* eligible unit, repeat. Never fight over a row.
5. Build the unit to its verify gate. Commit work incrementally (green only). **If a unit runs
   long, push something at least every 90 minutes** — a green WIP commit, or a heartbeat edit to
   your own row. That push is what tells other sessions you are alive; without it your row is
   reclaimable after 90 minutes (see the staleness rule).
6. Finish: set status `done` + commit SHA, or `in-progress` + concrete continuation notes (what's left, where, how to verify), or `blocked` + reason (e.g. founder gate). Push with rebase-retry (up to 4 attempts).
7. Parallelisation is expected: overlapping sessions hold different rows. Never edit another session's non-stale claimed row.

## Staleness — when you may reclaim someone else's row (W54)

A flat window cannot tell a slow holder from a dead one. On 2026-08-09 builder-A hit a model
limit mid-claim on W51; because the only rule was "6 hours", its dead row idled *both* routines
for over two hours and two firings found nothing to do. The rule is now **evidence-based**:

| Holder has… | Window | Measured from |
|---|---|---|
| pushed nothing since claiming | **90 minutes** | the claim time |
| pushed a commit / heartbeat | **6 hours** | its last push |
| visibly failed (model limit, dead session) | **none — reclaim now** | — |

Progress, not elapsed time, is the liveness signal: a holder that has pushed something has proven
it is running and keeps the long leash; one that has pushed nothing has proven nothing. Both are
checkable from `git log` alone, so no session needs to ping another.

Two deliberate refusals: a claimed row whose timestamp is **missing or unreadable** is left alone
and flagged (stealing a live claim is worse than an idle row), and a claim timestamped in the
future is never reclaimed on clock skew.

The normative definition is `classifyClaim` in `src/loop/claims.ts`, simulated against this
incident's real timestamps in `src/loop/claims.test.ts`. Change the rule there and here together.

## Fleet — builders run on different models (W54)

W51 §Process: a single-model fleet is a single point of failure. When builder-A exhausted its
model limit, it did not fail loudly — it held a claim and stopped, which is the worst failure
mode for a ledger-locked loop.

- **Run the builders on different models.** One provider limit, one model deprecation or one
  capacity incident must not be able to stop every builder at once.
- **Record the model in the session id** where it helps (`builder-A`, `builder-B`, …) and name
  the model in the session log entry, so a post-mortem can see which model stalled.
- **A builder that hits its limit is `knownDead`** — any other session may reclaim its row
  immediately under the staleness rule; there is nothing to wait for.
- **Routines outlive sessions.** A routine firing into a dead session is wasted, so a fleet of
  two on one model is worth less than a fleet of two on two models.

> archetype label were one idea rendered as two orphans with a ~250px dead band between them and the
> bottom-pinned CTA (every viewport, caused by `margin:auto` on `.voice-prompt` claiming all free

> overflowed the headline — rationale left in globals.css; a real desktop composition needs the shell
> widened for all stages and deserves its own unit. Gate: 58 files / 501 tests, audit gate PASS.

Projects from a measured sample and is explicit about WHICH quantities scale, which is the whole unit: LINEAR = sends, SMS spend, total compute (each practice's work is independent). Both properties are tested by projecting to 500 and 1000 and asserting one doubles while the other is identical. Assumptions live IN THE OUTPUT, not in a comment, because a projection whose assumptions are invisible gets quoted as a measurement — and the report's first body line says "This is a projection, not a measurement". Names the three things that would break linearity (shared lock, single job queue, one provider rate limit across the fleet), none of which is modelled. The hash is RECOMPUTED on replay, never trusted from the event — otherwise a version could be published under an identity it does not have and the audit trail would name the wrong document. Criteria are carried OPAQUELY: this module hashes and versions them and never looks inside (evaluation is W120, escalation semantics W121, withdrawal W128), which is what lets the content stay gated while the machinery ships; the criterion shape gives symptom inference nowhere to hide — no free-text expression, no threshold, just a recorded fact code and present/absent. **A MUTATION CHECK EARNED ITS KEEP**: removing the supersession guard from `pathwayAt` changed NO test, because the guard was compensating for a wrong selection rather than expressing the rule — with two versions published at the SAME INSTANT it returned null, reporting no pathway in force when one plainly was. `pathwayAt` now asks the replay's own state using the same predicate the invariant test asserts, so implementation and test cannot drift, and the new case fails against the old code. A criterion MOVED BETWEEN LISTS appears TWICE, as a removal and an addition, because moving a rule from inclusion to exclusion INVERTS what it does to a patient and one line would invite that to be skimmed as a tidy-up. When nothing changed it SAYS SO rather than rendering an empty section: those are different claims and the second reads as "nothing important". A cancelled appointment puts the rebook back on the REFERRING practice, since the receiving one has nothing booked and no obligation to chase. Fixed to latest-by-date, with two DIFFERENT reports sharing that date reported as AMBIGUOUS rather than resolved by position (W111's rule: two matches are ambiguous, never the first one), surfaced on the console as "check which one stands"; an identical duplicate is NOT a conflict, because calling it one sends a practice chasing a difference that does not exist. **SHARPENED RULE FOR Q12** (in the doc): for any fold, ask what the SORT KEY is and construct two records that TIE on it; if there is no principled winner the tie is a FINDING — pick the safe side and write down why, or report the ambiguity. The two fixes took different branches, and which is right depends on whether a safe side exists: for acceptance it does, for two disagreeing clinical letters it does not. Fixed with a chain-position tie-break — not invented information, since the state machine already declares a referral must exist before anything happens to it — with different-dated events untouched (asserted), and the genuinely-unrecoverable cases (same kind twice, same-day cancel-and-rebook) FILED rather than guessed at. **BOTH CONFIRMED BY WRITING THE FAILING TEST FIRST, AND BOTH FIXES PROVEN NON-VACUOUS BY REVERTING THEM AND WATCHING THE NEW TESTS FAIL AGAIN.** The class is now EIGHT instances in this tree; report states the rule at full strength plus two new corollaries (if code DETECTS an ambiguity, check the code that RESOLVES it reads the detection; "stable sort" guarantees positions, not meaning). 16 controls confirmed clean incl. G8 — nothing in src/app/scripts names a model endpoint (W153's scanner). 7 items carried to Y4, 5 of them founder gates. Verify: pnpm verify green, 1753 tests / 140 files, audit:gate PASS |
